ParlaMint-AT_2004-07-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00073_d7e3807	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-07-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00073_d7e3822	Ladies and gentlemen! I would first like to briefly explain the differences between structured and close cooperation. They know that structured cooperation is a cooperation within the European Union and that it is a question of Member States fulfilling the military capabilities of demanding missions, that is, Member States entering into a firmer commitment with regard to missions with demanding requirements. As far as closer cooperation is concerned, it is about ensuring mutual assistance, with the EU Constitution providing that the national and legal situations of the Neutrals are addressed or taken into account. In the future, we will have to carry out a national evaluation. The National Security Council and, of course, Parliament will be involved.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-07-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00073_d7e5520	I have always said that two factors are important. The first factor is the progress of the reform, which we are very committed to. The second factor is the extent to which we will be active at the border in the future. I insist that we must make a completely new reform of the basic defence service, which is adapted to the current threat situation. That means you have to look at basic training. It is currently the case that classical basic training lasts two months and arms training takes two months. That is why I believe that a reduction in the training phase will be necessary so that we can have a corresponding use phase for certain tasks. In this regard, we will present a serious approach, and Parliament will ultimately decide.

For this we have taken action – and we will do so too!

Instead of that, we have to take note today, we support two and a half major radio operators. Granted, these too have their merits, they have – we have already heard it – saved many little ones life to this day. However, this would have been the task of policy to shape the living conditions for private radios in such a way that entrepreneurs – young entrepreneurs in most cases – can take private money into their hands and invest, pay corresponding salaries and, above all, train young people for a profession that really has a future. The state should support these entrepreneurs – this is what it has rejected today, and that is why we cannot accept this law.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-07-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00073_d7e11520	Madam President. High house! In my statement on this joint social rights amendment package by the Ministry of Social Affairs and Health at the beginning, I may also briefly comment on those areas which were introduced by Mr Bundesminister Haupt into this Social Rights Amendment Act. I can also apologise to him: He is at an EU Ministerial Conference in Maastricht, and I promised him to address his areas briefly. However, I would like to talk, of course, of course, about my areas and about the questions on the farm package and, above all, on the obligation of the chief physician. This social rights amendment package includes, among other things, a change to a five-year reimbursement obligation in respect of the report to the Federal Government on the expenses of replacement time accounting and migration in the area of pension insurance, i.e. administrative simplification; second, the exemption from the administrative burden resulting from the EU extension and the planned introduction of a pension account from the administrative cost cover; thirdly, the possibility of using the exemption from compulsory insurance for small business as early as 60. Life-year – up to now it was the 65th; I think that this is a significant improvement for a generation of women, and therefore I thank you for that –; fourth, the implementation of the VfGH knowledge of the opt-out of the voluntary insurance professionals; fifth, the simplification and acceleration of the widow's or widow's pension procedure through the transmission of income data stored by the Federal tax authorities – which is also very important for many women, therefore also a thank you here – and sixth clarifications on the derivation of survivors' pensions and on the exclusion effect of early retirement from the disability, occupational incapacity and incapacity for work, i.e. things that are in themselves administrative simplification, but do not concern major areas. Let me now turn to the three most important points that we have discussed here today. First of all, these are the farmers' package, second, the chief physician's duty, and I would also like to address the repair of the loan repayment with regard to the VfGH knowledge, because I think this is a good example of how to solve something in agreement with all those concerned. First, let me come to the Farm Financing Package or Farm Consolidation Package. This, too, is closely linked to the abolition of essential provisions on the compensation fund by the Constitutional Court because it was clear that the Farmers' Insurance Fund is in a very difficult financial situation and, also as far as payment capacity is concerned, was in an urgent situation. What made me very confident was that the farmers recognized this very well and were already sitting with me on the day of their findings and asked for a joint consolidation in the package, expressed their willingness to immediately put in place all the necessary measures that they can take on their own but at the same time said: We can't do this alone! We need the support of the federal government, and we need the solidarity of the others. Why? – We know that the farmer’s health insurance fund of all health insurance companies has the worst structure: 143,000 active people face 138,000 pensioners! In other words, the ratio of active to pensioners is almost 50 to 50, and the income sizes are not very high, especially for many ancillary farmers. The farmer's health insurance fund has been very, very strict in recent years. For example, they are already attached to the ASVG as far as the benefits are concerned. They have put in place all the consolidation measures or many consolidation measures they have been able to put in over the past few years – to the difference between other health insurance companies that are always talking about being peanuts, that is not stupid. And now we have come to understand that some of these “Peanuts” at the Vienna regional health fund make up all €7 million. That was also the difference! We have given the farmers the same insight as we did at the Vienna regional health fund, and this survey report is now available and is already taken into account in this package. And I am very glad that the farmers have decided exactly what is happening in all their bodies. You really went through all the federal states here. That's how I imagine self-management in the best sense! And of course they also came and said: But that also needs support from the federal government! We have therefore granted here a contribution to tobacco tax, with the splitting of the amount for the self-employed and for the ASVG insured, i.e. the non-self-employed, and this is included here. What is it about now? – The farmers were ready – and this is not the first step, Mr Scheuch, but the second step towards harmonisation in this legislative period – to raise health insurance contributions to 7.4 percent. You know, one year ago, we harmonised the contributions of workers and employees to 7.3 plus 0.1, so 7.4 percent. The farmers are now doing this and have, of their own initiative, come up with this proposal. We are missing only more of the third step, namely the reduction of the contribution of the industrial economy from 9 to 7.4 percent. We will still have to do this in this legislative period, only: There is also a need for a step-by-step approach, because otherwise this health insurance scheme, which is now well-prepared, may be in trouble. Therefore: That must also be considered, but we will keep this in mind. In this legislative period, at least as far as the level of contributions is concerned, we would have taken an important, very large step in three stages. And then, Mr Oellinger, I am entirely of your opinion, the benefits must also be harmonised. This will not be quite so easy, because the first ones who will shout will be the doctors, because of course they have contracts different from those of the commercial economy, for example in the ASVG or elsewhere. Here too, in the sense of self-management, one must find a solution that is the same for all. ParlaMint-AT_2004-07-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00073_d7e13551	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! The main purpose is to extend the time limit for the submission of applications for benefits from the Funds. Additional costs are incurred by extending the deadline for the application, but in view of the efforts of the Republic of Austria to reach a solution to all open questions relating to the compensation of the victims of National Socialism. I would like to take this opportunity – so that we can see the dimension of what is being compensated – how extensive this greatest robbery of history that the Nazis have carried out in our country was. It is a total of 200 billion schillings that have been robbed, and that is a huge amount of money when it comes to accounting for this one billion dollars, which is now about to be restored, and it is not at all in relation to people who spent three years, four years in concentration camps or did slave labor. That's what I'm asking myself: How can these modest amounts be adequately compensated? Austria has thereby done away with a material and legal obligation, which is in order, the ethical and moral dimension of the murder of 6 million Jews is legal and also not to make up for material compensation, because in truth the uniqueness of this crime is lacking in any category of thinking. One also has to add self-critically that the question of repair in the Republic of Austria lasted a total of 60 years. For a long time, a co-indebtedness of the Austrian people, which would have resulted in a duty to repay, was definitely rejected, because this was a long time confirmation for the victims of the Austrian state as the first victim of Hitler-Germany. So what do you do if you were a victim of yourself? In fact, this is also a part of Austrian history and part of Austrian reparation if you want to do so. In addition, for many years a comprehensive moral, social and cultural rehabilitation of the Jews, but also all those who were persecuted for other reasons that had to emigrate, among them numerous scientists, philosophers and intellectuals, did not take place. This always becomes painfully aware of you, as it was recently, when you learned about the death of a famous chemist of Austrian provenance in England and had to take note of it and actually became aware of what the Austrian Republic had to take and take away. In this context, we should therefore thank all those who have made a positive effort to rework history. I remember, for example, the late President of the Federal Republic of Kletyl. In the past, I have always appreciated his merit, which he has done in this context, because this is a very, very important thing. More serious than this material-technical side, however, is, in my view, the spiritual challenge. And since, I believe, we are only at the beginning of the work, how many unfortunate incidents of recent times show in Germany and in other parts of Europe, where there is simply a continuity, xenophobic, racist and national socialist thinking, and where one has to say with regret that this continent has for a long time not mastered the spiritual restitution and that many uncontrolled patterns of thinking and behaviour have continued, and also in Austria – almost atavist from the past – have recently broken up. Therefore, we must also take this into account and remember that there is still much to be done here, regardless of the positive that has been done, regardless of what has happened in the past, for example, that there is a day of commemoration against racism, that there are also many other initiatives that promote critical self-reflection, whereby I believe that only critical self-reflection offers an opportunity to learn from the mistakes of the past, to draw the corresponding consequences – and that this is the only thing that works and which is essential as a prerequisite for the defence of xenophobia and xenophobia. I would also like to say this here, regardless of the relatively modest material reparation, regardless of the fact that I want to acknowledge it, but also to see the dimensions of what really happened in this country.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-07-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00073_d7e13930	We're now on the 5th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. First speaker is Mrs Dr. Fekter. - Please, you're talking.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-07-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00073_d7e13954	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! I would also like to welcome the listeners! With this novel, we will modernise the bureaucracy, especially those of the highest courts. It is true that the Constitutional Court is subject to considerable burdens: 7,000 inputs per year, and the number does not increase, but it does not decrease significantly either. It is therefore necessary for Parliament to support the highest courts legally and to implement improvements proposed. On the one hand, this novelle is designed to eliminate the ambiguities in the concepts that have repeatedly led to a high level of clarification, but also to use new technologies. New for the complainants – i.e. for the rightful population – is that Tele-Banking is allowed. You know, ladies and gentlemen: We abolished the stamp marks a long time ago. However, the deposit and the evidence for the deposit have meant bureaucracy. It is therefore justified to use the new modern methods, so that not only transfers by post and credit institutions are permitted, but Tele-Banking is also allowed. It is a modern legal approach, which we naturally support with the novelle. The notary overload of the administrative court has already been eroded by several measures. The independent administrative Senates – the UVS –, the independent financial Senate – UFS, or the recently established body, the Bundesasylsenat, do relieve the administrative court in some areas, but structural reform is nevertheless necessary. In the Austrian Convention, this has been discussed very intensively in its own working group, and there is broad consensus that we need national courts for the administration sector. It is not yet entirely agreed whether it should only become nine national courts or whether the system should be established by nine national courts plus an administrative court for the Federal Republic of First Instance. It is intended to enable the 133 authorities to be integrated and the Administrative Court, which we now have, to be only a purely revisional court. Until then – and this is the work of the Convention – we must be content with small steps. And such a small step is today's novelle.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-07-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00073_d7e15000	The next speaker is Mr Pack. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-07-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00073_d7e15018	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! As Mr Reheis mentioned earlier: Not only in Austria, but in all the institutions of the European Union, there is agreement that books are not just a commodity of commerce, they are cultural values and guarantee freedom of expression and freedom of teaching and research. Not only in Austria, but also in many other EU countries, there is therefore a price fixing of books. The Austrian model was in many cases used as a model. As mentioned above, it is based on the conviction that only price-bound books ensure that the number of Publications do not sink – and, of course, cultural diversity remains. The publishers are encouraged not only to place on the market commercially successful books, such as “My friend, the Dachshund”, but also to place culturally valuable and smaller editions on the market. Thus, the final effect can be guaranteed that the high quality of our literature remains. Moreover, price fixing also ensures a comprehensive supply for consumers with the books, as it allows a higher number of small bookstores to be claimed on the market, especially in the individual districts of Austria. What is particularly important, however, is that it is forgotten again and again – Mrs Wolfmayr mentioned it briefly –: Price fixing is not only important for the dealers and publishers, but it is especially important for the writers, with price fixing especially important for young writers, because they would have no price fixing, the even bigger problem, suitable, even a publisher. For this reason, one can only support this four-party request, and if one looks at it and sees the two small sentences mentioned in it, one must say: This means a lot to the local bookstores, to the publishers, to consumers. Therefore: In this case, there is nothing better than to agree to this proposal.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-07-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00073_d7e15066	The next speaker is Mrs Fleckl. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-07-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00073_d7e15085	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! Ladies and gentlemen! Books are not only merchandise, books are also cultural goods. Therefore, publishers and bookstores are not only entrepreneurs but also cultural intermediaries. This aspect therefore makes it clear that the book trade must not be viewed only in commercial terms. Book pricing is a historically grown phenomenon that has proved itself to be the best for decades. And that's how it's supposed to stay! The book pricing law was considered a transitional solution a few years ago, just before a complete market liberalisation. The fact that this is not seen this way today has several reasons: On the one hand, even with two years of delay, Germany has also adopted a law on this subject, which is based on the Austrian regulation – Germany is our most important ally in the EU when it comes to book pricing – and on the other hand, the EU has now recognised the national book pricing systems, unless they are cross-border price commitments. Experience from other countries that have given up book pricing shows that while a small part of the new publications will be cheaper, the other books will be expensive or no longer appear for cost reasons. Conclusion: The offer goes down; the total turnover remains the same, because the prices are printed on those books which are also included in the This was the result of an increase in the price of the product concerned. And we certainly do not want such developments in Austria. What is the situation in the country of free market economy, in the USA, where there is no book price binding? – Out of the large urban centers, bookstores have become very rare, as large chains of traditional bookstores increasingly crowd out. Only the following comparison should give us some thought: The number of new annual publications per capita in Germany is two to three times as high as in the USA. It is precisely this level that must be maintained! I think the book pricing law has proved itself in recent years and is compatible with Community law. Four years ago, this law was still devalued as a “relict ” by some in this House, today a proven instrument that takes into account the cultural characteristics of the book. I am pleased that this proposal will be supported by all the political groups today. Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-07-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00073_d7e15142	The next speaker to speak is Mr Deputie's friend. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-07-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00073_d7e15160	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! I too would like to speak on book pricing. Four years ago, i.e. in 2000, Parliament passed a very important law on the survival of publishers, bookstores and authors. At that time the law was still in existence until 30. ==References== Today, it is a question of abolishing this term – and that is a year before its expiry. This law aims to prevent large bookshop chains from pressing the book price and thus small publishers, smaller bookstores, under massive pressure. – Of course, we could now ask ourselves whether price fixing is appropriate in the age of globalisation and liberalisation. The answer to this question is, in my opinion, a clear yes, because: Books are not some commodity, they are a cultural property! Even though more and more information is being retrieved from the Internet, books will always have a very high priority in our society. Although the book of the Book of Often was said dead, it is of course still an essential part of our cultural life. There is a very high diversity in the book sector. Whether scientific literature, classical works, criminal novels or philosophical works: For every taste there is something in it! Book pricing makes a significant contribution to maintaining this diversity, as it is given today, by helping so-called bestsellers to finance the production and distribution of not so common literature. Smaller and medium-sized book trading companies could no longer cope with the new market situation. The law on book pricing, setting the 5 percent limit on discounts, was an important measure to counter it. I am therefore pleased that this request is supported by all parties to increase the time-limitation of book pricing.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-07-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00073_d7e15221	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Mr State Secretary! High house! The book is an important democratic and ideal cultural asset of our society, which promotes fundamental values of our constitution, namely freedom of expression and information. It is therefore very important to protect the Book of Culture in this sense, not to regard it as a common commodity and to subject it to market-oriented competition. All the long-standing, partly emotional, controversies of opponents and supporters of book pricing have shown clearly the importance of the book of cultural goods both in our national and European society. The book award ceremony has proved to be a guarantee of a culturally very valuable further development of Austrian literature. Smaller Austrian publishers and smaller bookstores, which, in particular, have distanced themselves from the principle of viewing the book as commercial bulk goods, were thus undoubtedly secured in their inventory. Imported books are also subject to book pricing. This guarantees that international bestseller literature cannot be sold at dumping prices in the large trading chains. Therefore, smaller bookstores, which cannot or can only adapt to these lowest prices, have an interesting opportunity to offer us consumers the opportunity to buy literature from less well-known, but especially Austrian authors. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! It is also particularly important that many places of work and training were obtained, in an industry where neoliberalism could not cause any damage, an industry in which the small merchant did not have to deviate the large trade chains. The Austrian literature and the Austrian publishing houses as well as smaller bookstores can be preserved and further promoted. Here important cultural values were put before profit interests. Market economic interests have been put behind important social principles. This is a victory for our culture, a victory for the book, a victory for elementary values of our constitution. The book pricing law has proved successful, and the European Parliament is very positive about this law. I am pleased that all the political groups are working together. The abolition of this temporary period paves the way for a positive development for the cultural property book. - Thank you.

First of all, it should be said that the teaching profession, of course, requires the full ability of a teacher to act, because, in addition to the provision of classical teaching content, it is also a matter of supervision of students, that risks can be identified in good time and that one can also intervene in student conflicts. – In each case, it is necessary to examine the extent to which it is possible to actually exercise such a teaching office. On the other hand, we also have the fact that during their time as a teacher, such as an accident, people who already practice the teaching profession suddenly suffer a disability, but then, of course, they can continue to practice their teaching profession. There is, of course, a certain degree of inequality, including what opportunities arise. We want to oppose this inequality treatment and therefore propose in a four-party resolution that, in the course of the re-design of the educational academies towards educational colleges, it will be examined to what extent people with disabilities are not allowed to complete teaching in order to be used accordingly. I believe that this is a very important step which we are expressing. In the second block, which my colleagues will address later, one point is very important, namely the question of the optical recognition of the important tasks of teachers in compulsory education, in particular the chairman of the trade union public service Fritz Neugebauer and the chairman of the compulsory school teacher union Walter Riegler have been very committed to making visible the important training of graduates of compulsory education. I would now like to refer to the Federal Law, which amends the Federal Law on Agriculture and Forestry. Multifunctional agriculture and forestry requires comprehensive and well-founded training for the training of different combinations of work, as well as in the field of middle management, which requires an adaptation to the curriculum. The new curriculum will now enter into force in autumn 2004. In view of the multilingual background of the European Union and the increasing international orientation of the agricultural sector, foreign language competence is given a special importance. In concrete terms, this means that in all disciplines except agriculture and nutrition, in addition to the subject matter of "living foreign language", the "second living foreign language" or an English specialist seminar will be introduced alternatively. With this seminar, even the level “Independent User B2” and in individual skills can be reached the level of the “Professor User C1”. The amendment to this government proposal is also very brief. You know, just a few weeks ago, in the framework of the Agricultural Law Change Act Article 6, we decided to merge some federal institutions and research institutes in Wieselburg and Raumberg to create new, strong and efficient regional education and research clusters: the Bundesanstalt für Landtechnik in Wieselburg with the higher Bundesanstalt Francisco Josephinum and the higher Bundeslehringanstalt in Raumberg with the Bundesanstalt für Alpenlande Landwirtschaft. I would also take up the idea of introducing new FH courses in order to perhaps further strengthen these research and education clusters. This amendment is now about creating the legal basis for this reorganisation. The amendment of this federal and forestry school law is now expected to be parallel to the Federal Law on Federal Institutions. In January 2005 the Commission adopted a proposal for a Directive on the approximation of the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to the approximation of the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to the approximation of the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States To the Academies' Law of Study. I am pleased that this has now succeeded in awarding the degree of diploma, and from this point of view I very much congratulate the new diploma educators on this title, especially the teachers from the Most Quarter, especially those from my district of Scheibbs. On the contrary, they have helped to reduce sham-scribing, and they have made the study what it is supposed to be: a training opportunity that needs full use and, if State support becomes necessary, it will also be provided.

Why are we at tuition fees? – That has several reasons. First of all, there are fundamental reasons: We say about social democracy that we do not want to solve certain risks and certain opportunities individually, but rather solidarity. That's the risk of getting old, the risk of getting sick, the risk of losing work, but that's also the chance of education. We believe that we should resolve these questions in a solid and non-individual way. Access restrictions are also to be kept as low as possible, particularly in the field of education. The tuition fees are a barrier, also a social and monetary barrier to access to study, and that is why we are against it. There are other reasons why we are against it. For example, it has been proved that tuition fees are bad and have negative effects. Mr Kuntzl has already pointed out the Kolland study, which was financed by the Ministry and then not pushed out for a long time – for a good reason: because the judgment of this Kolland study clearly shows that those fears that we have had also occurred, namely that from a remote level of education, access to universities is reduced, that children from families from non-educational shifts are less likely to study and then, of course, less often to complete a study than was the case before the study fees were introduced. The second is that the study fees force students to work more, and that spending more time on the job of course also means spending less time on studying. This can certainly not be in the sense of what you always appeal to, namely, to study faster. The question, however, is not only what we are saying about tuition fees, but it is also the question of what the people concerned are saying. There are some studies, last but not least one from the Austrian University of Applied Sciences, a survey by SORA, which has carefully examined how satisfied students with tuition fees et cetera are. [[Abg. May. Molterer: This is about as if you ask taxpayers for tax exemptions!]] There is a very clear picture, namely that 60 percent of students consider the tuition fees to be unjustified and, after all, 5 percent of students believe that the introduction of study fees would have improved the study conditions. 50 percent think they're just as bad as before, and 33 percent think they got even worse. If we still think about the pictures of mass lectures in Vienna's cinemas, then I can only give this 33 percent in truth right. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] [[Intervocations at the ÖVP.]] It's also exciting what we see when we look at these numbers more closely. There is a group that pays tuition fees but gets them refunded. How does this group actually stand at tuition fees? – The tension is that 58 percent – i.e. only 2% less than the group as a whole – say that they reject tuition fees. Theoretically, they would not have affected them in themselves, but nevertheless this group is also against the tuition fees. One last thing about what students say about the future, because one hears over and over again that now it is also thought to set up further restrictions on access, not only such a monetary way, but also others: 70 percent of students are massively opposed to access restrictions and are opposed to them. We too are voting against it and, of course, we are still in favour of abolishing the tuition fees. - Thank you.

We should probably also consider whether the constitutional provisions that we have to comply with, namely that we define exactly in which languages and where these projects are being carried out for consultation, are still timely, whether the public edition could not be replaced by Internet access, whether this really needs to be decided so explicitly in each individual case and whether this could not be done automatically. All of this should also be considered in connection with changes to the Constitution. It would also be necessary to consider whether, given the importance of foreign policy and, in particular, EU issues, Parliament's work in this context, i.e. the reports on the activities of COSAC and reports from other interparliamentary bodies, as was the case in the past, should again come to the House today in the form of its own report in order to be able to debate Parliament's attitude and its representatives on EU issues again. These small groups such as COSAC, for example, and other delegations, the Council of Europe, WEU, NATO et cetera, have many opportunities to adopt a behaviour for Parliament, but there is no mandate. Parliament still has a debate and a feedback on the attitude of these delegations. I think these proposals are a good reason to think about how we could better bring EU templates here in the future. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-07-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00073_d7e21066	We're on the 27th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. I am now opening the debate. It is the first honourable Member to report on the matter. Hakl. Voluntary speaking time limit: 4 minutes. Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-07-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00073_d7e21093	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Mr State Secretary! – State Secretary Morak is going, but still there. – Dear ladies and gentlemen! In Sudan, the worst humanitarian crisis we have to deal with in this world is the present one. This is perhaps not yet known to each of the colleagues in the House. The main issue of the conflict is the prevention of raw materials. Two black African rebel groups fight against the militias supported by the Sudanese government. Rape, expulsion and forced recruitment of child soldiers are on the agenda. In particular, the aid organizations can hardly rush to the aid because one can no longer distinguish friend from enemy. With this request, we want to strengthen our foreign minister so that she continues to work within the EU, at all international levels and at the UN to ensure that the pressure on the Government of Sudan remains so great and becomes so great that the conflict in particular does not go beyond Chad and that refugees can safely return to their country. Ladies and gentlemen! Our plane leaves at 7:00. Leave You're telling me, We all go to a well-deserved summer break, as I believe. But if some of you, some of us who are doing well, pay one of the many organizations in favour of Sudan for some euro, then your money is as well invested as rarely anywhere. Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-07-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00073_d7e21127	The next speaker was Mrs Bayr. Voluntary speaking time limit: 4 minutes. Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-07-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00073_d7e21145	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! In any event, this four-party request underlines that we are all in the opinion here, the need to increase political pressure on Sudan. The conflict in Darfur has political reasons, and I think it will only be possible to resolve it politically. My colleague Bettina Stadlbauer and I invited on behalf of the SPÖ women at the beginning of the week to a round table on Sudan to pool the help, exchange information and create synergies. I think it was very successful too. Many organizations have followed our invitation, for example “Pharmacies without Borders”, UNICEF, the Austrian Red Cross, the Caritas, the Austrian-Sudanese Society, the Darfur Committee, UNIFEM, Amnesty, CARE, the Sudan Platform and some others. It was a very fruitful exchange on how we can help all together best. One issue was particularly central, namely the question of the coordination of emergency and disaster aid. A member of the NGOs has described very dramatically that, although there are the means and the possibilities to pull people out of the ruins, then, if they are safe, when they are saved, is for the next time until development aid is used, often no money is available, and one can simply let people starve themselves in between. From my point of view, it is incredibly necessary that there really be two clearly defined budget posts for emergency aid and disaster aid, that there are transparent award criteria for these funds, and that there should be, above all, clear responsibilities at a ministry. Especially in the event of disaster, we all know, helps the double who helps quickly. It is therefore also important to Kongruenz, and it is simply incredibly important to eliminate all bureaucratic ambiguities on this issue. Help is really urgent, and it is often about hours or days. Especially now the rainy season in Sudan is in place, and it is really urgent to do something. I would also like to say a few words about this draft UN resolution. I believe it would be important to extend the arms embargo to all Sudan. The militias could not commit their crimes to the population at all without the control, financing and hidden support of the Sudanese government. “Measures” are addressed in the draft resolution. I think it is important to specify what measures are to be taken. For example, when it comes to freezing Sudanese foreign accounts, I think this is a very smart step, because it is mainly the Sudanese government and not the population. The specification of these “measures” would be of great concern to me. Just a short while on the role of the Sudanese government: Unfortunately, your announcement to disarm the militias has not yet been followed. - They're still armed. This fact does not necessarily make the President's seven-point programme much more credible. Unfortunately, it is still a fact that aid organisations are being prevented from gaining access to the crisis area. It's almost a genocide in time. The government is all too often hiding behind the new peace agreement in the south and is trying to end the problems and the conflict in Darfur. If nothing happens, then the brutalities committed to the population of Sudan – especially in Darfur, and it is about millions of people – are probably just a prelude to an even greater humanitarian disaster. I think that the four-party application is a good first step and a basis for increasing Austrian pressure as well as European and UN printing. I'm glad we can bring him in together. – Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-07-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00073_d7e21233	Madam President. Mrs. Foreign Minister! Conflicts only take place for us when they are seen over the media and running over the television screens, and there are currently other priorities. This is a pity, because human suffering, human rights violations are serious everywhere and not dependent on whether camera teams are filming or not. Unfortunately, the mechanisms are always the same: Governments, neighbouring countries and any alleged uncontrollable militias are involved, and tens of thousands of innocent civilians remain on the track as they are now in Sudan: 10,000 dead, 100,000 refugees, over 1 million affected. Although the UN sends some Commissioners and observers and adopts resolutions that are not complied with, the European Union is not at all concerned with this question, and we can only, here in the Austrian Parliament, helplessly adopt resolutions – at least that! That's what we can do. It is far too little, but we should nevertheless set a sign of solidarity with these victims, with the people and do everything at the level of the European Union and the United Nations that not only is condemned, but there are also sanctions against Sudan and against all the regimes that are actively or indirectly responsible for such human rights violations. Behind all of this is the economic interest as often as it is. It's also about oil supplies. Mrs. Foreign Minister! Take this unanimous resolution as a mandate and as support for your initiatives and do so in order to put pressure on the European Union and on the United Nations so that this human, humanitarian disaster can be ended as soon as possible!

First: The US Secretary of State Colin Powell was in Sudan and had talks with the Sudanese government and put them under great pressure. Discussions have already been circulated for a draft resolution based on Chapter VII of the UN Charter so that additional pressure on Sri Lanka could be exerted. UN Secretary-General Kofi Annan joined the United Nations on 29 July. ==References== It was held in Sri Lanka in July to reach agreement with the Sudanese Government on the following measures which I would like to briefly list to you: The Sudanese government committed itself, among other things, to allowing freedom of movement for all humanitarian workers active in Darfur. – That was an important demand. It also committed itself to abolishing restrictions on the import and use of humanitarian aid materials. – That was a second important requirement. It also committed itself to ensuring that individuals and groups who have committed human rights violations would, as far as possible, be brought to justice, and in the field of security, to immediately disarm Janjaweed militias. In addition, a mechanism has been set up, called Joint Implementation Mechanism, which is deliberately directed by the Sudanese Foreign Minister and by the UN Secretary-General's Special Representative, to see that the Sudanese Government also has a word. At the same time, I can say that it is not the case that the European Union is not dealing with this issue. At the last Council, we have already adopted conclusions on this, and also at the next Council on Monday, there will be conclusions which also threaten that certain further measures will come from the European Union – that is, some sanctions – if the Sudanese Government does not now give in to this pressure that is being exerted. I myself have – as was the case with my House before – and that is what we have told you, ladies and gentlemen. – always kept in touch with the Sudanese ambassador. I have also contacted him myself and, of course, passed on exactly this pressure, which is a concern to all of us. Finally – and this is something specific – we immediately provided €200 000 for immediate assistance, which is used in two areas: the UN refugee relief work and the World Food Programme. And I'm sure we're... If there is going to be a donor conference, it will certainly be a little bit more ‘pledged’. In addition, the ECHO programme of the European Union, within which €14 million are made available, is of course also equipped with Austria. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-07-09-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00073_d7e21477	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! It has already been said a lot about this resolution, so I can now confine myself to a few things. Everything is missing: water, food, medicine. It is also lacking in the good will of the Government in Sudan, and it is therefore positive that the Community of States has decided to act together. It is also positive that this motion for a resolution is based essentially on a few central points, namely that one may help to stop the fights immediately, that aid organisations should have access to the Civil War area, that those who have committed crimes against humanity be brought to a court, that all militias are disarmed, and that there is effective assistance for refugees on the ground. I am pleased that there is this motion for a resolution. Therefore, the European colonial powers cannot simply escape their responsibilities in Sudan. Thank you.
